### D.1 OpenThoughts: recovery and recognition

This section gives the protocols and complete results for recovery after
errors
(§[4.2](#S4.SS2 "4.2 Solution improves recovery after errors ‣ 4 RQ1: Source ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models"))
and error recognition
(§[4.3](#S4.SS3 "4.3 Error recognition remains limited ‣ 4 RQ1: Source ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")).

#### D.1.1 Cohort construction

For each model scale, we draw 12 reference-free Thinking-ON samples per
OpenThoughts problem before any source-conditioned generation. The RQ1
mixed-outcome pool contains problems with 1–11/12 correct samples,
yielding 646, 183, and 164 problems at Qwen3-1.7B, 4B, and 8B. This
broader pool differs from the 1–5/12 Frontier bucket used in RQ3. It is
frozen before error-boundary selection, and screening answers use the
canonical evaluator.

#### D.1.2 Recovery after errors

For each mixed-outcome problem, we select one correct and one incorrect
non-truncated trajectory while minimizing their token-length difference
and randomize display order. GPT-5.6-Sol and Claude Opus 4.8
independently mark the earliest 128-token chunk containing a specific
recoverable mathematical error without seeing terminal outcomes.
Incompleteness, exploration, and a different valid strategy do not count
as errors. Consensus produces 296, 113, and 78 eligible pairs; 50 per
scale are selected across four bins for the number of tokens before the
error (0–1,023, 1,024–2,047, 2,048–4,095, and at least 4,096) before any
Qwen condition is evaluated.

Recovery begins from a fixed prefix ending after both judges’ marked
error chunks and draws four matched continuations under problem-only,
shuffled-reference, and privileged-solution conditions using four
prespecified decoding streams, with the frozen teacher in Thinking ON at
temperature 1.0. The shuffled-reference condition supplies a worked
solution to a different problem in place of the privileged solution.
GPT-5.6-Sol and Claude Opus 4.8 independently assign task-level
valid-solution labels to all 1,800 continuations, with a blinded pass
adjudicating disagreements. A continuation must repair or abandon the
inherited error and reach the requested result. Ambiguous responses
count as incorrect. Confidence intervals resample source problems while
preserving the matched continuations.

| Model | Prefixes / clusters | Problem only | Shuffled | Privileged | Privileged $`-`$ problem | Privileged $`-`$ shuffled | Trunc. P/S/V |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|
| Qwen3-1.7B | 50 / 46 | 48.50% | 41.50% | 74.50% | +26.00 \[+16.35, +36.06\] | +33.00 \[+21.50, +44.61\] | 0.0/0.5/0.0% |
| Qwen3-4B | 50 / 46 | 66.00% | 51.00% | 88.50% | +22.50 \[+11.27, +34.31\] | +37.50 \[+24.53, +50.00\] | 0.0/0.0/0.0% |
| Qwen3-8B | 50 / 46 | 63.00% | 53.50% | 88.50% | +25.50 \[+15.87, +36.00\] | +35.00 \[+23.21, +47.34\] | 0.0/0.0/0.0% |

Table D2: OpenThoughts task-level recovery from 50 prefixes containing
verified errors per scale. Each arm has four continuations per prefix.
Brackets are paired 95% source-problem-cluster bootstrap intervals.
P/S/V denotes problem-only/shuffled/privileged.

Privileged solutions support recovery after verified errors.
Privileged-solution recovery reaches 74.50–88.50% and exceeds shuffled
references by 33.00–37.50pp. Every interval excludes zero. Task-level
grading rejects invalid proofs and continuations that do not repair the
prefix, yet the privileged-context advantage remains large.

#### D.1.3 Error recognition

We construct 200 audited prefix pairs per scale. Each contains two
prefixes from the same trajectory: a valid prefix ending before the
earlier judge mark and an erroneous prefix ending after the later mark.
These panels span 200, 172, and 156 unique source questions at 1.7B, 4B,
and 8B, respectively. Repeated states are kept together in uncertainty
estimates. The frozen teacher judges whether each prefix is correct so
far, with and without the privileged solution, using deterministic
Thinking-ON decoding at temperature 0. It is instructed not to solve the
full problem and to end with exactly one line, either “VERDICT: ERROR”
or “VERDICT: NO_ERROR_VISIBLE”. ERROR requires a substantive
mathematical error, invalid inference, or false claim already visible in
the prefix. A response parses only when a closed thinking block is
followed by exactly one prescribed verdict. A missing close, missing
verdict, or multiple verdicts counts as incorrect, and parse rate is
reported separately. Accuracy on valid prefixes (specificity) measures
correct acceptance. Accuracy on erroneous prefixes (recall) measures
correct flagging. Overall prefix accuracy (balanced accuracy) averages
the two, and pair accuracy (joint accuracy) requires both judgments in a
pair to be correct. All metrics use the same 200 pairs. An always-accept
baseline has 100% accuracy on valid prefixes, 0% on erroneous prefixes,
50% overall prefix accuracy, and 0% pair accuracy. The response cap is
min(38,912, 40,960 minus rendered input tokens).

| Model | Pairs | Valid N/R | Error N/R | Overall N/R | Pair N/R | Pair R $`-`$ N | Parse N/R | Trunc. N/R |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|
| Qwen3-1.7B | 200 | 56.0/65.0% | 23.0/25.5% | 39.5/45.2% | 9.5/13.0% | +3.50 \[-2.00, +9.50\] | 78.8/78.8% | 3.0/0.8% |
| Qwen3-4B | 200 | 78.5/82.0% | 14.5/13.0% | 46.5/47.5% | 9.0/6.5% | -2.50 \[-7.50, +2.50\] | 89.0/87.0% | 2.5/5.5% |
| Qwen3-8B | 200 | 86.0/89.0% | 18.5/16.5% | 52.2/52.8% | 14.0/12.5% | -1.50 \[-6.50, +3.50\] | 94.0/97.0% | 0.5/0.2% |

Table D3: OpenThoughts explicit error recognition. Valid and Error
report accuracy on valid and erroneous prefixes. N/R denotes problem
only/privileged solution. Pair R $`-`$ N is in pp. Intervals use 50,000
paired source-question-cluster bootstrap resamples, preserving repeated
pair states from the same source question.

Privileged solutions do not reliably improve recognition. Accuracy on
erroneous prefixes remains only 13.0–25.5%. Privileged context raises
overall prefix accuracy by +5.75pp \[+0.50, +11.00\] at 1.7B, +1.00pp
\[-3.25, +5.25\] at 4B, and +0.50pp \[-3.00, +4.25\] at 8B. Intervals
for pair accuracy include zero at every scale. Parse rates of 78.8–97.0%
and truncation rates of 0.2–5.5% rule out the response cap as the main
explanation. Unparsed responses count as incorrect, and parse rates
differ by at most 3pp between conditions within each scale.

#### D.1.4 Blind response audit

We apply an outcome-blind response audit to OpenThoughts.
Appendix [D.2.5](#A4.SS2.SSS5 "D.2.5 Blind response audit ‣ D.2 Hendrycks MATH Level 5: solving and replications ‣ Appendix D RQ1: Source ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
applies the same protocol to Hendrycks MATH Level 5. Each teacher mode
contains 225 response pairs: 150 automatically identified
problem-only-wrong to privileged-correct pairs (50 per scale) and 75
wrong-to-wrong controls (25 per scale). Candidate order is randomized.
GPT-5.6-Sol sees the problem and worked solution as mathematical
evidence but not the generation condition or automatic-verifier outcome.
We report mechanism labels only when the judge independently confirms
that the problem-only response is incorrect and the privileged response
is correct.

The mechanism label identifies the dominant change in the successful
response: a local repair preserves the original route, global strategy
selection replaces it, verification or final-answer correction changes
only the terminal check or answer, close reproduction follows
distinctive reference steps, and search pruning or supplied fact
captures other direct reductions in search. Deployment is labeled
self-contained when the successful response justifies its reasoning on
its own and privilege-dependent/direct when distinctive imported steps
are not independently supported.

| Audit dimension | Label | Thinking ON (n = 40) | Thinking OFF (n = 66) |
|----|----|----|----|
| Mechanism | Local repair | 14 (35.0%) | 22 (33.3%) |
| Mechanism | Global strategy selection | 9 (22.5%) | 19 (28.8%) |
| Mechanism | Verification or final-answer correction | 13 (32.5%) | 8 (12.1%) |
| Mechanism | Close reference reproduction | 4 (10.0%) | 8 (12.1%) |
| Mechanism | Search pruning or supplied fact | 0 (0.0%) | 9 (13.6%) |
| Deployment | Self-contained derivation | 35 (87.5%) | 58 (87.9%) |
| Deployment | Privilege-dependent or direct | 5 (12.5%) | 8 (12.1%) |

Table D5: Mechanism and deployment labels among blind-confirmed
OpenThoughts improvements. Categories within each audit dimension are
mutually exclusive.

OpenThoughts improvements are usually not copies of the privileged
solution. Local repair and strategy selection together account for 23/40
(57.5%) confirmed improvements under Thinking ON and 41/66 (62.1%) under
Thinking OFF. Close reproduction accounts for only 10.0% and 12.1%. The
successful response is judged self-contained in 87.5% and 87.9% of
cases. The Hendrycks MATH Level 5 audit uses a slightly different label
set, so we compare only the shared close-reproduction and self-contained
labels: on that corpus, close reproduction is the largest category and
roughly half of improvements remain privilege-dependent or direct
(Appendix [D.2.5](#A4.SS2.SSS5 "D.2.5 Blind response audit ‣ D.2 Hendrycks MATH Level 5: solving and replications ‣ Appendix D RQ1: Source ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")).
Because the blind judge confirms only 40/150 and 66/150 automatically
selected wrong-to-right pairs, these proportions are descriptive
sensitivity evidence. The matched outcome contrasts in main-text
Tables [2](#S3.T2 "Table 2 ‣ 3 Experimental Setup ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")-[2](#S3.T2 "Table 2 ‣ 3 Experimental Setup ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
remain the primary evidence.

![Refer to caption](2611.16064v1/image2.png)

Figure D2: Two OpenThoughts examples from the blind response audit under
Teacher Thinking OFF. The top shows a self-contained local repair that
removes one invalid case. The bottom shows a privilege-dependent
re-solve that imports the reference-specific net-profit setup. Examples
are condensed for legibility.

### D.2 Hendrycks MATH Level 5: solving and replications

We use Hendrycks MATH Level 5 for the hard-problem solving analysis in
§[4.1](#S4.SS1 "4.1 Solution improves hard-problem solving ‣ 4 RQ1: Source ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
and for cross-dataset replications of recovery and recognition. The
section reports high-precision cohort construction, hard-problem
solving, recovery after errors, explicit error recognition, and a blind
response-transition audit.

#### D.2.1 Cohort construction

We start from the cleaned 2,242-problem Hendrycks MATH Level 5 corpus
described in
Appendix [B.2](#A2.SS2 "B.2 Hendrycks MATH Level 5 ‣ Appendix B Data and common setup ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models").
For each frozen Qwen3 checkpoint, 12 reference-free Thinking-ON
screening samples are generated per problem at temperature 1.0, top-p
1.0, top-k disabled, and seed 0. An answer-equivalence screen identifies
125, 62, and 53 candidate 0/12 questions at Qwen3-1.7B, 4B, and 8B. To
obtain a high-precision task-level cohort, GPT-5.6-Sol and Claude Opus
4.8 independently judge all 2,880 candidate responses against the
problem and verified solution. They agree on 98.65% of responses. Every
disagreement is resolved by a separate blinded adjudication pass. We
retain a question only when all 12 responses are judged incorrect,
yielding 111, 44, and 35 questions. The cohorts are locked before any
fresh source-condition outcome is inspected.

For the error-boundary analyses, we begin from model-relative
mixed-outcome problems with both correct and incorrect screening
trajectories. A deterministic priority rule selects one correct and one
incorrect candidate per problem while favoring non-truncated, closed,
shorter responses. This yields 180, 80, and 79 candidate pairs.
GPT-5.6-Sol and Claude Opus 4.8 independently audit randomized
candidates in 128-token chunks; 149, 69, and 53 pairs satisfy the
consensus criteria. Before any Qwen recovery or recognition output is
examined, 50 pairs per scale are selected deterministically while
preserving subject and error-position coverage.
Table [D1b](#A4 "Appendix D RQ1: Source ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
summarizes this attrition.

#### D.2.2 Hard-problem solving

On each audited 0/12 cohort, the frozen teacher produces four fresh
Thinking-ON samples under problem-only, subject- and length-matched
shuffled-reference, and privileged-solution input at temperature 1.0,
top-p 1.0, and top-k disabled. Each shuffled donor has the same subject,
comes from a different question with a different gold answer, and
minimizes reference-token-length difference without donor reuse within
scale. Remaining ties use the lower source index. GPT-5.6-Sol and Claude
Opus 4.8 independently assign task-level valid-solution labels to all
2,280 fresh responses against the problem and verified solution. The
rubric accepts equivalent answer forms but, for proof or construction
tasks, requires a valid derivation rather than a terminal-answer match
alone. The judges agree on 88.55% of responses, and a separate blinded
pass resolves every disagreement. All responses are present and remain
within the native context window.

| Model | n | Problem only | Shuffled | Privileged | Privileged $`-`$ problem | Privileged $`-`$ shuffled |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|
| Qwen3-1.7B | 111 | 2.93% | 1.13% | 40.54% | +37.61 \[+31.31, +44.14\] | +39.41 \[+33.11, +45.95\] |
| Qwen3-4B | 44 | 1.14% | 1.70% | 64.20% | +63.07 \[+51.14, +74.43\] | +62.50 \[+50.57, +73.86\] |
| Qwen3-8B | 35 | 0.00% | 1.43% | 58.57% | +58.57 \[+46.43, +70.71\] | +57.14 \[+45.71, +68.57\] |

Table D6: Hendrycks MATH Level 5 task-level valid-solution accuracy on
independently audited 0/12 cohorts. Each condition uses four fresh
samples per problem. Brackets are paired 95% question-bootstrap
confidence intervals.

Privileged solutions provide strong, problem-specific source capability.
Privileged-solution input sharply raises valid-solution accuracy
(40.54–64.20%), while problem-only and shuffled-reference accuracy
remain at or below 2.93%. Every privileged-solution contrast excludes
zero, showing that the gain depends on information matched to the
current problem rather than solution-like context alone.

#### D.2.3 Recovery after errors

Using the 50 consensus pairs per scale selected in
Appendix [D.2.1](#A4.SS2.SSS1 "D.2.1 Cohort construction ‣ D.2 Hendrycks MATH Level 5: solving and replications ‣ Appendix D RQ1: Source ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models"),
recovery begins from a fixed prefix ending after both judges’ marked
error chunks. The frozen teacher generates four Thinking-ON
continuations, one per prespecified decoding stream, under problem-only,
subject- and length-matched shuffled-reference, and privileged-solution
conditions, with temperature 1.0, top-p 1.0, and top-k disabled. The
primary privileged-minus-shuffled comparison holds the prefix, prompt
wrapper, and decoding seed fixed while changing whether the supplied
solution matches the problem. Unlike the OpenThoughts recovery audit,
which uses task-level judges, final answers here are scored with the
canonical exact-answer evaluator, so Hendrycks MATH Level 5 recovery
rates are not directly comparable to
Table [D2](#A4.SS1.SSS2 "D.1.2 Recovery after errors ‣ D.1 OpenThoughts: recovery and recognition ‣ Appendix D RQ1: Source ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models").
Intervals resample source-problem clusters, and every selected sequence
respects the native context window.

| Model | Prefixes / clusters | Problem only | Shuffled | Privileged | Privileged $`-`$ problem | Privileged $`-`$ shuffled | Trunc. P/S/V |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|
| Qwen3-1.7B | 50 / 46 | 26.00% | 22.00% | 92.50% | +66.50 \[+55.29, +76.96\] | +70.50 \[+60.11, +80.10\] | 0.5/0.0/0.0% |
| Qwen3-4B | 50 / 41 | 39.00% | 27.50% | 97.50% | +58.50 \[+46.76, +69.68\] | +70.00 \[+57.87, +80.80\] | 1.0/0.0/0.0% |
| Qwen3-8B | 50 / 41 | 37.00% | 36.50% | 96.00% | +59.00 \[+47.92, +69.39\] | +59.50 \[+47.73, +70.28\] | 1.0/0.5/0.0% |

Table D7: Hendrycks MATH Level 5 recovery after errors. Each of 50
prefixes containing an audited error has four matched continuations per
arm. Contrasts in pp show 95% source-problem-cluster bootstrap intervals
from 50,000 resamples. P/S/V denotes problem-only/shuffled/privileged.

Task-matched context remains useful after a verified error. The
privileged-minus-shuffled contrasts in
Table [D7](#A4.SS2.SSS3 "D.2.3 Recovery after errors ‣ D.2 Hendrycks MATH Level 5: solving and replications ‣ Appendix D RQ1: Source ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
reach 70.50, 70.00, and 59.50pp across the three scales. Shuffled
references do not improve over problem-only continuations, ruling out
extra solution-like text and the teacher wrapper as sufficient
explanations.

#### D.2.4 Error recognition

Error boundaries are fixed before the Qwen3 judgments. GPT-5.6-Sol and
Claude Opus 4.8 independently inspect randomized candidate trajectories
with the problem and worked solution as mathematical evidence but
without terminal outcomes. Each labels the reasoning as valid so far,
containing a recoverable mathematical error, or ambiguous. A recoverable
error must be a visible false claim or invalid inference that could
still be retracted. Incompleteness, exploration, and a different valid
strategy do not count. Each judge identifies the earliest 128-token
error chunk and provides a quotation and explanation. We retain only
consensus errors.

We select 50 trajectories per scale, stratified by subject and error
position. From each trajectory, we form a valid prefix ending before the
earlier annotated error chunk and an erroneous prefix ending after the
later chunk. The frozen teacher judges each prefix with and without the
privileged solution using deterministic Thinking-ON decoding at
temperature 0. It must end with exactly one line, either “VERDICT:
ERROR” or “VERDICT: NO_ERROR_VISIBLE”. Accuracy measures correct
acceptance of valid prefixes and correct flagging of erroneous prefixes.
Pair accuracy requires both judgments to be correct. The response cap is
min(38,912, 40,960 minus rendered input tokens), and confidence
intervals resample matched problem pairs.

| Model | Pairs | Valid N/R | Error N/R | Overall N/R | Pair N/R | Pair R $`-`$ N | Parse N/R | Trunc. N/R |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|
| Qwen3-1.7B | 50 | 42/52% | 40/42% | 41/47% | 12/22% | +10.00 \[-4.00, +24.00\] | 83/84% | 2/2% |
| Qwen3-4B | 50 | 72/66% | 30/38% | 51/52% | 18/16% | -2.00 \[-14.00, +10.00\] | 92/97% | 2/1% |
| Qwen3-8B | 50 | 72/76% | 40/20% | 56/48% | 24/12% | -12.00 \[-26.00, +2.00\] | 99/98% | 0/1% |

Table D8: Hendrycks MATH Level 5 explicit error recognition. Valid and
Error report accuracy on valid and erroneous prefixes. N/R denotes
problem only/privileged solution. Pair R $`-`$ N is in pp. Intervals for
pair accuracy use 20,000 paired problem-bootstrap resamples.

We find no evidence that privileged context improves recognition on
Hendrycks MATH Level 5. In
Table [D8](#A4.SS2.SSS4 "D.2.4 Error recognition ‣ D.2 Hendrycks MATH Level 5: solving and replications ‣ Appendix D RQ1: Source ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models"),
pair accuracy changes by +10, -2, and -12pp across scales, and every
confidence interval includes zero. Accuracy on valid and erroneous
prefixes also changes direction across scales. Parse rates range from
83% to 99%, and truncation rates from 0% to 2%, so missing or truncated
judgments do not explain the result. Together,
Tables [D6](#A4.SS2.SSS2 "D.2.2 Hard-problem solving ‣ D.2 Hendrycks MATH Level 5: solving and replications ‣ Appendix D RQ1: Source ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")–[D8](#A4.SS2.SSS4 "D.2.4 Error recognition ‣ D.2 Hendrycks MATH Level 5: solving and replications ‣ Appendix D RQ1: Source ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
reproduce the main asymmetry: the privileged solution greatly improves
solving and recovery, but we find no evidence that it identifies the
error boundary.

#### D.2.5 Blind response audit

Final-answer accuracy does not reveal how privileged-solution responses
differ from problem-only responses. We therefore conduct an
outcome-blind audit of independently generated complete answers under
Teacher Thinking ON and OFF. The 225 prespecified response-pair slots
contain 75 slots per scale and were selected using Teacher-ON recorded
outcomes. All 225 judgments completed under Teacher Thinking OFF and 219
under Teacher Thinking ON. The latter 219 are the slots available in
both modes. Response order is randomized, and GPT-5.6-Sol sees the
problem and worked solution as mathematical evidence but not the
generation condition or automatic-verifier outcome. Mechanism
proportions condition on pairs for which the blind judge independently
confirms that the problem-only response is incorrect and the privileged
response is correct. Because the responses do not share a prefix, the
labels describe differences between complete answers rather than a
causal repair of one trajectory.

The mechanism label identifies the dominant response change: local
repair preserves the original route, new strategy replaces it, supplied
fact imports a decisive intermediate result, close reproduction follows
distinctive reference steps, and other collects search-pruning,
final-answer, or mixed changes. Deployment is labeled self-contained
when the successful response supplies sufficient independent support and
privilege-dependent/direct when it does not.

| Teacher | Matched slots | Confirmed | Local | New strategy | Supplied fact | Close reproduction | Other | Self-contained | Privilege/direct |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|
| Thinking ON | 219 | 105 | 12 (11.4%) | 23 (21.9%) | 24 (22.9%) | 35 (33.3%) | 11 (10.5%) | 48 (45.7%) | 57 (54.3%) |
| Thinking OFF | 219 | 80 | 11 (13.8%) | 21 (26.3%) | 7 (8.8%) | 34 (42.5%) | 7 (8.8%) | 39 (48.8%) | 41 (51.3%) |

Table D9a: Hendrycks MATH Level 5 blind-audit labels among
judge-confirmed wrong-to-right pairs. Percentages condition on the
Confirmed column. Categories are descriptive because slots were selected
using Teacher-ON recorded outcomes.

| Teacher | Model | Problem-only tokens | Privileged tokens | Reduction | Confirmed |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|
| Thinking ON | Qwen3-1.7B | 17,949 | 10,927 | 39.1% | 33 |
| Thinking ON | Qwen3-4B | 18,693 | 10,817 | 42.1% | 36 |
| Thinking ON | Qwen3-8B | 17,900 | 11,033 | 38.4% | 36 |
| Thinking OFF | Qwen3-1.7B | 1,980 | 1,289 | 34.9% | 24 |
| Thinking OFF | Qwen3-4B | 2,558 | 1,537 | 39.9% | 30 |
| Thinking OFF | Qwen3-8B | 2,414 | 1,461 | 39.5% | 26 |

Table D9b: Hendrycks MATH Level 5 response length and blind-confirmed
improvement counts for the 219 slots completed in both teacher modes.
Token columns are means over matched slots.

Close reproduction is the largest single category. It accounts for
35/105 confirmed improvements (33.3%) under Teacher Thinking ON and
34/80 (42.5%) under Teacher Thinking OFF. Same-strategy local repair
accounts for only 12/105 (11.4%) and 11/80 (13.8%). Overall, 54.3% and
51.3% of improvements import or reproduce reference-specific steps
without enough independent support to stand alone. Privileged responses
are also 35–42% shorter than problem-only responses at every scale in
both modes. These descriptive results show that high source accuracy on
Hendrycks MATH Level 5 often reflects direct use of hidden information
rather than independent reasoning.

![Refer to caption](2611.16064v1/image11.png)

Figure D4: Two Hendrycks MATH Level 5 examples from the blind response
audit. The top shows a same-route local repair. The bottom shows the
more common pattern of replacing the original setup with
reference-specific steps. Examples are condensed for legibility.

Hendrycks MATH Level 5 replicates the solving-recognition gap. The
frozen teacher can exploit a privileged solution both from scratch and
after an error, but privileged access does not reliably help it identify
the error boundary.

### D.3 Execution notes

OpenThoughts. All 1,800 planned OpenThoughts recovery generations are
present and receive two independent task-level judgments, with
disagreements resolved by blinded adjudication. Ambiguous labels count
as incorrect in the reported estimates. The recognition panel contains
four judgments per pair state (valid/erroneous prefix $`\times`$ problem
only/privileged solution), for 2,400 judgments in total. All retained
sequences remain within the native context window, and failed attempts
are excluded by scheduler status alone.

Hendrycks MATH Level 5. All 2,880 screening responses, 2,280 fresh
original-question responses, 1,800 recovery continuations, and 600
recognition judgments are present in the final Hendrycks MATH Level 5
analysis. Every screening and fresh original-question response receives
two independent task-level judgments, followed by blinded adjudication
of disagreements. The response-transition audit contains 225 completed
Thinking-OFF judgments and 219 completed Thinking-ON judgments. The 219
matched slots are used in
Tables [D9a](#A4.SS2.SSS5 "D.2.5 Blind response audit ‣ D.2 Hendrycks MATH Level 5: solving and replications ‣ Appendix D RQ1: Source ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")–[D9b](#A4.SS2.SSS5 "D.2.5 Blind response audit ‣ D.2 Hendrycks MATH Level 5: solving and replications ‣ Appendix D RQ1: Source ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models").
To enforce the Qwen3 native 40,960-token context window, the recovery
audit uses 103 protocol-matched replacements covering two previously
missing 50-row arm/seed cells and three previously over-length rows. The
recognition audit uses five replacements covering four missing rows and
one over-length row. The largest retained total sequence length is
exactly 40,960 tokens. Replacement sources are chosen from
scheduler-success states before response text is inspected, and all
recovery outcomes use the paper's canonical exact-answer evaluator.

## Appendix E RQ2: Encoding

This appendix follows the three RQ2 tests in the main text.
Section [E.1](#A5.SS1 "E.1 Target shift ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
documents target-shift scoring and its Hendrycks MATH Level 5
replication.
Section [E.2](#A5.SS2 "E.2 Correctness ranking ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
reports verified correctness-ranking dynamics, using OpenThoughts for
Thinking-OFF and Hendrycks MATH Level 5 for Thinking-ON.
Section [E.3](#A5.SS3 "E.3 Branch choice ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
gives the branch-selection protocol and results on both corpora.

### E.1 Target shift

#### E.1.1 Construction and scoring

For each model and student mode, we retain questions with at least one
automatically scored correct and incorrect response among 12
reference-free frozen-Base samples generated at temperature 1.0, top-p
1.0, and top-k disabled. Thinking-OFF trajectories come from
OpenThoughts and Thinking-ON trajectories from Hendrycks MATH Level 5.
One trajectory of each type is chosen within question to minimize
response-length mismatch, and both must contain at least 1,024 clean
response tokens. This yields 200, 183, and 168 OpenThoughts pairs for
Thinking-OFF trajectories at Qwen3-1.7B, 4B, and 8B, and 200 Hendrycks
MATH Level 5 pairs per scale for Thinking-ON trajectories. The first 512
response tokens form the fixed context and the following 512-token
suffix is scored. At every suffix position, we compute the exact
full-vocabulary teacher-to-student FKL using the problem-only and
privileged-solution teacher templates in
Appendix [B.5](#A2.SS5 "B.5 Thinking modes and prompts ‣ Appendix B Data and common setup ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models").
Overall FKL is averaged within trajectory and then across questions.
$`\Delta\text{FKL}_{\text{ref}}`$ subtracts the problem-only value from
the privileged-solution value on identical tokens. The paired problem
bootstrap uses 20,000 replicates.
Table [E1a](#A5.SS1.SSS1 "E.1.1 Construction and scoring ‣ E.1 Target shift ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
adds confidence intervals to the overall estimates in
Table [4](#S5.T4 "Table 4 ‣ 5.1 The solution changes the teacher’s targets ‣ 5 RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models").

Token-family taxonomy. We decode each scored token, strip surrounding
whitespace, and lowercase it before assigning one mutually exclusive
family. Epistemic markers are the exact normalized tokens *wait,
actually, check, hmm, perhaps, maybe, alternatively, seems, might,* and
*likely*. Tokens containing a digit, a TeX delimiter, or a Unicode
mathematical symbol are math/numeric. Alphabetic ASCII and other
non-ASCII text are lexical. Tokens composed only of punctuation or
symbols form the punctuation/symbol family. Whitespace, special tokens,
and control tokens form the final family.
Table [E1b](#A5.SS1.SSS1 "E.1.1 Construction and scoring ‣ E.1 Target shift ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
reports conditional per-token means for both cohorts. These values do
not sum to Overall because each is normalized by the number of tokens in
that family.

| Thinking OFF | Thinking ON | 0.0423 \[0.0381, 0.0471\] | 0.0350 \[0.0320, 0.0381\] | 0.0315 \[0.0285, 0.0345\] |
| Thinking ON | Thinking OFF | 0.1382 \[0.1302, 0.1463\] | 0.1042 \[0.0975, 0.1109\] | 0.0993 \[0.0928, 0.1062\] |
| Thinking ON | Thinking ON | 0.0761 \[0.0678, 0.0850\] | 0.0689 \[0.0621, 0.0761\] | 0.0583 \[0.0519, 0.0650\] |

protocol. Requiring mixed outcomes in both modes leaves 91, 101, and 108
matched questions at Qwen3-1.7B, 4B, and 8B. Both trajectory modes then
use the same fixed questions, minimum-length-mismatch pairing rule, and
scored positions.

Tables [E1b](#A5.SS1.SSS1 "E.1.1 Construction and scoring ‣ E.1 Target shift ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
and
[E3](#A5.SS1.SSS2 "E.1.2 Hendrycks MATH Level 5 replication ‣ E.1 Target shift ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models").
These results establish that the reference changes the target, but they
do not establish that the change points toward correct reasoning.

### E.2 Correctness ranking

#### E.2.1 Cohorts and metric

GPT-5.6-Sol and Claude Opus 4.8 independently evaluate both trajectories
in all 1,151 automatically selected correct-incorrect pairs from
Appendix [E.1](#A5.SS1 "E.1 Target shift ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models"),
with a separate blinded pass resolving disagreements. We retain a pair
only when the final labels preserve its correct-incorrect ordering; 894
pairs remain. The Thinking-OFF cohorts come from OpenThoughts and
contain 174, 150, and 139 pairs at Qwen3-1.7B, 4B, and 8B. The
Thinking-ON cohorts come from Hendrycks MATH Level 5 and contain 156,
145, and 130 pairs. Excluded pairs are not relabeled into a new
contrast.

Each trajectory score averages the per-token readout over a fixed
512-token suffix. Exact FKL measures the full teacher-to-student
divergence. The executed loss clips each vocabulary summand at 0.05
before aggregation, matching the training objective. AUROC treats the
incorrect trajectory as the positive class, gives ties half credit, and
equals 0.5 at chance. This trajectory-level ranking is a coarse
diagnostic rather than a necessary condition for useful token-local
learning.
Section [E.3](#A5.SS3 "E.3 Branch choice ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
provides the more local test at matched error states. Step 0 uses one
frozen-Base estimate. Later checkpoints average three prespecified
training seeds. Confidence intervals use 20,000 paired problem resamples
at Step 0 and hierarchical seed-and-problem resamples thereafter.

#### E.2.2 Training dynamics

We rescore the verified pairs at Steps 0, 20, 40, 60, 80, and 100. The
student distribution comes from the corresponding checkpoint, while the
teacher remains the frozen Thinking-ON Base model. At Step 0, the
problem-only readout is constant, yielding AUROC 0.5 under the tie
convention above.
Tables [E4a](#A5.SS2.SSS2 "E.2.2 Training dynamics ‣ E.2 Correctness ranking ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")–[E4b](#A5.SS2.SSS2 "E.2.2 Training dynamics ‣ E.2 Correctness ranking ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
report Step-100 estimates with confidence intervals, and
Figure [E1](#A5.SS2.SSS2 "E.2.2 Training dynamics ‣ E.2 Correctness ranking ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
plots every checkpoint with its interval.

privileged-minus-problem interval in
Tables [E4a](#A5.SS2.SSS2 "E.2.2 Training dynamics ‣ E.2 Correctness ranking ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")–[E4b](#A5.SS2.SSS2 "E.2.2 Training dynamics ‣ E.2 Correctness ranking ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
includes zero. The benchmark gains in
Figure [1](#S1.F1 "Figure 1 ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
therefore do not coincide with a reliable privilege-specific ranking of
incorrect trajectories.

### E.3 Branch choice

#### E.3.1 Protocol

From each audited prefix ending before an error, the frozen Thinking-ON
student sees only the problem and prefix. Candidate proposals use seeds
100–115, temperature 1.0, top-p 0.95, and top-k 20. The first four
unique, full-length 128-token branches in seed order are retained. The
same frozen checkpoint then scores each branch at temperature 1.1 as a
problem-only student and as a privileged-solution Thinking-ON teacher.
The student selector chooses the branch with the highest student mean
log likelihood. The privileged selector chooses the branch with the
highest teacher-minus-student mean log likelihood. Each fixed branch
then receives four matched reference-free Thinking-ON continuations
using outcome seeds 0, 1, 2, and 3 under the same sampling settings. The
38,912-token response budget includes the prefix and branch, and all
sequences remain within the native 40,960-token context window.
Comparing sibling branches from the same state removes between-problem
difficulty, and every selected outcome receives blinded task-level
adjudication. Final answers are first scored by the paper's canonical
exact-answer evaluator. The main estimates use the blinded
full-population adjudication in
Section [E.3.2](#A5.SS3.SSS2 "E.3.2 Cohort construction and adjudication ‣ E.3 Branch choice ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models").
All branches, scores, and continuations are fixed before outcome
adjudication or selector comparison. State-bootstrap intervals use
50,000 replicates and preserve the four candidates and their matched
continuations as a unit.

#### E.3.2 Cohort construction and adjudication

State selection is frozen before branch generation. GPT-5.6-Sol and
Claude Opus 4.8 independently inspect one non-truncated Base trajectory
per problem, selected by a prespecified hash ordering, in 128-token
chunks and retain a state only when both identify a recoverable
mathematical error with at least 256 tokens of valid reasoning before
that error. No state is filtered using selector scores or downstream
outcomes.

Before completing the 4B and 8B outcome evaluations, we preregistered
the following interpretation rule: describe weak branch-level
information only if a positive OpenThoughts effect appears at two or
more Qwen3 scales. Otherwise describe any 1.7B result as scale-specific.
We report all six corpus $`\times`$ scale cells regardless of outcome
and treat cross-corpus agreement as stronger evidence than one interval
excluding zero.

Outcome adjudication. Because the automatic verifier misses many correct
answers on these continuations
(Table [E7](#A5.SS3.SSS2 "E.3.2 Cohort construction and adjudication ‣ E.3 Branch choice ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")),
final branch-success estimates replace automatic labels with a
task-level protocol. It evaluates all 4,928 unique rollouts selected by
either selector. GPT-5.6-Sol judges every response while blind to
corpus, model scale, selector identity, branch score, and automatic
label. The 1,374 responses for which this judgment disagreed with the
automatic label or was ambiguous were independently reviewed by Claude
Opus 4.8. A third blinded GPT-5.6-Sol pass resolved the 280
disagreements between the two model judges. The primary analysis
conservatively counts the remaining 163 ambiguous responses as incorrect
(Table [E5](#A5.SS3.SSS2 "E.3.2 Cohort construction and adjudication ‣ E.3 Branch choice ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")).
Table [E6](#A5.SS3.SSS2 "E.3.2 Cohort construction and adjudication ‣ E.3 Branch choice ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
reports the two alternative treatments.

Expanded-panel construction. The frozen expansion targets 150 source
states per corpus $`\times`$ scale cell, with at most one state per
source question. Every retained state satisfies the same dual-judge
error-consensus rule and supplies four unique, full-length candidate
branches before selector scoring. Candidate coverage removes one
OpenThoughts Qwen3-1.7B state, leaving 149. All 150 OpenThoughts
Qwen3-4B and 8B states remain. The available Hendrycks MATH Level 5
consensus pools retain 150, 68, and 59 states at 1.7B, 4B, and 8B,
respectively. No state is filtered using selector scores or downstream
outcomes.

Table E7: Dual-judge audit of 200 OpenThoughts branch continuations
labeled incorrect by the automatic verifier. A false negative is a
response the judges deem correct. The final column divides these counts
by non-ambiguous audited responses. The upper rows split the sample by
model scale and the lower rows split the same sample by selector
category. Wilson intervals use non-ambiguous judgments. The balanced
sample is descriptive rather than population-weighted.

Branch selection is not robust. The preregistered two-scale OpenThoughts
criterion is not met after full adjudication. Only the 1.7B OpenThoughts
interval strictly excludes zero. The Hendrycks MATH Level 5 4B interval
reaches zero at its lower endpoint and becomes unresolved when ambiguous
judgments receive half credit or are excluded. Under either treatment,
the OpenThoughts 1.7B estimate remains positive, while every other
interval includes zero. We therefore describe the detected advantage as
local and scale-specific rather than as a general branch-ranking
capability.

#### E.3.3 Verifier audit

We audit whether automatic answer-label errors could create the selector
contrast. Before inspection, we sample 200 responses labeled incorrect
from the expanded OpenThoughts experiment, balancing the three scales
and four selector categories: chosen by both selectors, only the student
selector, only the privileged selector, or neither. At most one response
is retained per audited error state. GPT-5.6-Sol and Claude Opus 4.8
independently receive the problem, reference solution, extracted answer,
and response, but not model scale, selector identity, or branch scores.
They judge mathematical equivalence, including option-label/value
equivalence. Raw agreement is 97.0%. A separate blinded pass adjudicates
the six disagreements.

Final selector estimates use full adjudication. In this balanced sample
of verifier-negative responses, the share judged correct ranges from
16.4% at 1.7B to 64.6% at 8B. The privileged-only and student-only
shares are 47.1% and 46.0%, a +1.06pp contrast with a bootstrap interval
of \[-18.71, +20.78\]. This interval is too wide to validate selector
differences of only a few pp, which motivated the full-population
adjudication in
Section [E.3.2](#A5.SS3.SSS2 "E.3.2 Cohort construction and adjudication ‣ E.3 Branch choice ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models").
Table [E7](#A5.SS3.SSS2 "E.3.2 Cohort construction and adjudication ‣ E.3 Branch choice ‣ Appendix E RQ2: Encoding ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
is therefore a diagnostic of the automatic verifier, not the source of
the paper’s final branch-success estimates.

## Appendix F RQ3: Transmission

Section [F.1](#A6.SS1 "F.1 OpenThoughts acquisition and thinking-mode audit ‣ Appendix F RQ3: Transmission ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
gives the OpenThoughts analyses of pass@8, repeated training, access to
the supplied solution, and thinking mode behind
Figures [3](#S6.F3 "Figure 3 ‣ 6.1 Repeated training raises pass@8 ‣ 6 RQ3: Transmission ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")-[3](#S6.F3 "Figure 3 ‣ 6.1 Repeated training raises pass@8 ‣ 6 RQ3: Transmission ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models").
Section [F.2](#A6.SS2 "F.2 Purification and retention ‣ Appendix F RQ3: Transmission ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
reports the longitudinal retention controls behind
Figure [6.3](#S6.SS3 "6.3 Purified OPSD limits late decline ‣ 6 RQ3: Transmission ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models"),
including Qwen mathematics, OLMo, and coding. Because the protocols
differ, all comparisons are made only within matched designs.

### F.1 OpenThoughts acquisition and thinking-mode audit

#### F.1.1 Question selection and training epochs

We screen all 3,200 examples separately for each Qwen3 scale and student
generation mode. Each problem receives 12 reference-free frozen-Base
samples under the same Thinking-ON or Thinking-OFF mode later used to
define that training condition. Unsolved contains problems with 0/12
correct samples. Frontier contains problems with 1–5/12. Student
Thinking-OFF runs therefore train on OFF-defined buckets, and Student
Thinking-ON runs train on ON-defined buckets. Buckets are model-relative
and are never pooled across scales or modes.

When a bucket contains at most 160 problems, we retain all of them.
Larger Unsolved pools are capped at 160 with a fixed hash ordering.
Larger Frontier pools are capped at 160 while balancing the five
Base-success counts. Every retained row has a non-empty privileged
solution, a valid reference answer matching the dataset answer, and a
verifier-compatible final answer. The worked-solution audit labels every
retained solution valid or minor-issue. None is invalid. Training
otherwise follows
Appendix [B.4](#A2.SS4 "B.4 Training ‣ Appendix B Data and common setup ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models").
We count training epochs as the number of examples processed divided by
the number of selected training questions: $`e=32s/N`$, where $`s`$ is
the optimizer step, 32 is the batch size, and $`N`$ is the training-set
size. We compare checkpoints nearest 1, 2, 4, 8, and 16 epochs within
each set.
Table [F1a](#A6.SS1.SSS1 "F.1.1 Question selection and training epochs ‣ F.1 OpenThoughts acquisition and thinking-mode audit ‣ Appendix F RQ3: Transmission ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
lists the numbers of selected questions and the exact optimizer steps.
These steps differ across sets, so epoch labels are approximate.

| Student mode | Model | Unsolved avail. / used | Unsolved steps | Frontier avail. / used | Frontier steps |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|

| Thinking OFF | Qwen3-8B | 156 / 156 | 5 / 10 / 20 / 39 / 78 | 399 / 160 | 5 / 10 / 20 / 40 / 80 |
| Thinking ON | Qwen3-1.7B | 106 / 106 | 3 / 7 / 13 / 27 / 53 | 111 / 111 | 3 / 7 / 14 / 28 / 56 |
| Thinking ON | Qwen3-4B | 107 / 107 | 3 / 7 / 13 / 27 / 54 | 45 / 45 | 1 / 3 / 6 / 11 / 23 |
| Thinking ON | Qwen3-8B | 97 / 97 | 3 / 6 / 12 / 24 / 49 | 35 / 35 | 1 / 2 / 4 / 9 / 18 |

#### F.1.2 Pass@8 and frequent success on Frontier questions

Each Thinking-ON-teacher, student-mode, and teacher-input condition is
trained separately on its corresponding bucket with three matched
training seeds. Every checkpoint is evaluated reference-free on the same
questions under both student thinking modes using eight fresh samples
per problem, temperature 1.0, top-p 1.0, top-k disabled, and a
38,912-token response cap.
Figure [3](#S6.F3 "Figure 3 ‣ 6.1 Repeated training raises pass@8 ‣ 6 RQ3: Transmission ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
reports pass@8 for matched student training and evaluation modes: the
number of questions with at least one correct answer among eight
samples, divided by the number evaluated. The Base points use a separate
eight-sample evaluation after the 12-sample screening. Checkpoint values
average the three training seeds equally, and shading shows the seed
range. The two thinking modes use separately selected question sets, so
their curves are not a same-question comparison.

retains the stricter Frontier analysis: the percentage of questions with
matched-mode Base below 7/8 that reach at least 7/8 correct responses

uses the separate dual-judge 0/16 rerun. Automatic-verifier Unsolved

#### F.1.6 Questions with no correct Base responses

To check initially unsuccessful questions more carefully, GPT-5.6-Sol
and Claude Opus 4.8 independently grade 16 frozen-Base samples per
Student Thinking-OFF question, with blinded adjudication of
disagreements. Eligibility requires all 16 samples to be incorrect and
none ambiguous, leaving 60, 24, and 11 questions at 1.7B, 4B, and 8B. We
rerun privileged-solution and problem-only training on these verified
question sets. At each checkpoint, the canonical answer-equivalence
evaluator scores 16 fresh samples per question. The dual-judge audit
determines which questions are included and does not relabel checkpoint
generations. Stable acquisition requires at least 14/16 correct samples
under matched Thinking-OFF evaluation. Thinking-ON results use the same
questions selected under Thinking OFF and therefore measure cross-mode
reachability rather than zero-start acquisition. The same evaluator
credits 55.6–79.2% of Thinking-ON samples on these questions
(Table [F1g](#A6.SS1.SSS6 "F.1.6 Questions with no correct Base responses ‣ F.1 OpenThoughts acquisition and thinking-mode audit ‣ Appendix F RQ3: Transmission ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")),
so its false negatives cannot by themselves explain the near-zero
Thinking-OFF rates.

### F.2 Purification and retention

#### F.2.1 Purified target

We implement Purified OPSD following [Shen et al. (2026)](#bib.bib9).
For every on-policy student prefix $`\hat{y}_{<t}`$, the same frozen
Base model is evaluated under three inputs: a teacher distribution
$`p_{T}(\cdot\mid\hat{y}_{<t},q,r)`$ with question $`q`$ and reference
$`r`$; a reference-only probe
$`p_{\mathrm{ref}}(\cdot\mid\hat{y}_{<t},r)`$ with the question removed;
and a clean Base distribution $`p_{0}(\cdot\mid\hat{y}_{<t},q)`$ with
the reference removed. The student
$`p_{\theta}(\cdot\mid\hat{y}_{<t},q)`$ is the only network evaluated
with gradients.

Over the full vocabulary $`V`$, the raw transferable signal is
$`\Delta_{it}(v)=\log p_{T}(v)-\log p_{\text{ref}}(v)`$. Subtracting its
vocabulary mean gives the centered signal $`\bar{\Delta}_{it}(v)`$, to
which we apply the soft clip of [Shen et al. (2026)](#bib.bib9),
$`\widetilde{\Delta}_{it}(v)=c\tanh(\bar{\Delta}_{it}(v)/c)`$, with
$`c=10`$. The target is
$`p_{\text{target}}=\operatorname{softmax}(\log p_{0}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{it}/\beta_{\text{PMI}})`$,
with $`\beta_{\text{PMI}}=1`$. Canonical Purified runs minimize the
response-position mean of the symmetric generalized Jensen–Shannon
divergence between $`p_{\theta}`$ and $`p_{\text{target}}`$ (mixture
coefficient 0.5), using the full vocabulary without an additional
token-level loss clip after vocabulary aggregation. The PMI correction
strength and JSD mixture coefficient are distinct hyperparameters.

Our Standard comparator is the paper-wide executed baseline: the
privileged teacher distribution is used directly with teacher-to-student
forward KL, with each vocabulary contribution upper-clipped at 0.05.
Thus the canonical Standard-versus-Purified curves compare the complete
executed recipes. The OLMo RawExact-versus-PurifiedExact pair separately
holds exact FKL fixed and changes only target construction.

#### F.2.2 Qwen mathematics and order controls

The Qwen analyses train Qwen3-1.7B, 4B, and 8B on the same audited
OpenThoughts corpus for 100 updates with three matched training seeds
per arm. Teacher and student rollout modes are Thinking ON. Evaluation
is reference-free Thinking ON Avg@12 on AIME 2024, AIME 2025, AIME 2026,
HMMT November 2025, and HMMT February 2026, with temperature 1.0, top-p
1.0, top-k disabled, and a 38,912-token response cap. We first
macro-average the five benchmarks within each seed and then average
seeds equally. The frozen Base is independently repeated with decoding
seeds 0, 1, and 2. Base-relative intervals use 50,000 independent
two-sample bootstrap resamples of the three training seeds and three
Base decoding seeds. Pure Standard and Purified runs are evaluated at
Steps 20, 40, 60, 80, and 100. The switch trajectories change objective
after Step 20. Matched-composition endpoints additionally reverse 20/80
and 80/20 update blocks.

#### F.2.3 OLMo exact-FKL control

We train OLMo-3-7B-Think ([Team Olmo, 2025](#bib.bib28)) on Hendrycks
MATH with three prespecified seeds per arm and evaluate every checkpoint
on the same five held-out mathematics benchmarks. RawExact and
PurifiedExact use the same prompt, temperature, LoRA configuration,
1,024-token training-rollout cap, 100-update schedule, and unclipped
exact forward-KL loss. Only target construction differs. PurifiedExact
retains the canonical three-pass, centered, tanh-clipped PMI target
($`\beta=1`$, $`c=10`$) but deliberately replaces the canonical JSD with
exact FKL. It is therefore a target-construction ablation, not a second
claim of exact paper replication. The released clipped Standard run
falls from 54.39% at Step 20 to 14.08% at Step 100. Comparing it
directly with canonical Purified would confound target construction with
loss and temperature, so those rows are supplementary diagnostics.

Table F3: OLMo-3-7B-Think five-benchmark Avg@12 across training on

Three-seed exact-FKL replication. Mean RawExact Avg@12 across Steps
20/40/60/80/100 is 54.98 / 53.50 / 52.44 / 52.69 / 52.35. Mean
PurifiedExact is 57.43 / 57.66 / 57.04 / 58.13 / 58.52. At Step 100,
RawExact scores 52.88 / 52.12 / 52.06 across seeds, while PurifiedExact
scores 58.32 / 59.16 / 58.09. The +6.17pp mean endpoint separation
therefore replicates across all three seeds.

#### F.2.4 Code replication

Qwen3-4B trains for 100 updates on 3,200 audited CodeContests problems
([Li et al., 2022](#bib.bib29)) with batch size 32, LoRA rank 64, and
Thinking ON for student rollouts and the privileged teacher. The public
student prompt and teacher solution/reprompt templates are
byte-identical to the released SDPO code ([Hübotter et al.,
2026](#bib.bib7)). The frozen teacher receives the judge-verified
reference program in the successful-attempt slot. Purified uses the
target defined by [Shen et al. (2026)](#bib.bib9) and summarized in
Section [F.2.1](#A6.SS2.SSS1 "F.2.1 Purified target ‣ F.2 Purification and retention ‣ Appendix F RQ3: Transmission ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models").
This is an SDPO-prompt-aligned domain transfer, not a replication of
SDPO’s data or RL objective. Evaluation is reference-free Thinking ON
with four samples per problem, temperature 0.6, top-p 0.95, top-k
disabled, and a 38,912-token response cap. LiveCodeBench v6 (LCBv6)
([Jain et al., 2025](#bib.bib30)) contains 131 problems and the disjoint
CodeContests held-out set contains 128. Base is one fixed decoding
evaluation. The 8K and 16K checkpoint summaries average three matched
training seeds. The 32K pilot uses one. Standard improves early but
degrades as truncation rises, while Purified remains near Base.
Tables [F4a](#A6.SS2.SSS4 "F.2.4 Code replication ‣ F.2 Purification and retention ‣ Appendix F RQ3: Transmission ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")–[F4b](#A6.SS2.SSS4 "F.2.4 Code replication ‣ F.2 Purification and retention ‣ Appendix F RQ3: Transmission ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")

## Appendix G RQ4: Expression

RQ4 uses a favorable correction-transfer stress test rather than tracing
the Standard OPSD target end to end: after training the student on
verified local corrections, we test on a question-disjoint held-out set
whether better target fit yields generation without privileged support.

Section [G.1](#A7.SS1 "G.1 Correction construction ‣ Appendix G RQ4: Expression ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
documents correction construction,
Section [G.2](#A7.SS2 "G.2 Protocol ‣ Appendix G RQ4: Expression ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
specifies training and evaluation,
Sections [G.3](#A7.SS3 "G.3 Longitudinal results ‣ Appendix G RQ4: Expression ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")-[G.4](#A7.SS4 "G.4 Step-48 generation endpoints ‣ Appendix G RQ4: Expression ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
report target fit, generation, and endpoint diagnostics, and
Section [G.5](#A7.SS5 "G.5 Matched controls ‣ Appendix G RQ4: Expression ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
gives the matched controls.

### G.1 Correction construction

We construct model-specific correction sets because the error states and
valid continuations differ between Qwen3-4B and Qwen3-8B. The source
pool contains mixed-outcome Thinking-ON OpenThoughts questions, for
which the frozen Base produced at least one correct and one incorrect
trajectory. GPT-5.6-Sol receives the problem, privileged solution, and
matched correct and incorrect trajectories, identifies a local
actionable error, and writes a repair instruction that does not reveal
the final answer. From the exact error-containing prefix, the
corresponding frozen Qwen3 teacher receives the privileged solution and
repair instruction and generates four candidate continuations with an
8,192-token completion cap. We retain verifier-correct, non-truncated
candidates, then apply a condition-blind self-containment audit with
GPT-5.6-Sol. Without seeing the source condition, the judge labels each
candidate self-contained, source-dependent, or invalid. The shortest
retained self-contained continuation per question defines the
model-specific correction target.
Table [G1](#A7.SS1 "G.1 Correction construction ‣ Appendix G RQ4: Expression ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
reports the filtering counts and the question-disjoint split.

| Model | Candidates | Self-contained | Source-dependent | Invalid | Selected | Train / held out |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|
| Qwen3-4B | 499 | 383 | 36 | 80 | 125 | 100 / 25 |
| Qwen3-8B | 415 | 320 | 28 | 67 | 102 | 82 / 20 |

Table G1: Correction-candidate filtering and question-disjoint split.
All candidates are verifier-correct and non-truncated. Counts precede
shortest-per-question selection.

This construction is deliberately favorable: every retained target
begins at an audited local error, reaches a verifier-correct answer, is
non-truncated, and passes the self-containment gate. The experiment
therefore tests whether training on available valid corrections supports
autonomous expression on held-out questions. It does not estimate how
often arbitrary model errors admit such a correction.

### G.2 Protocol

Across the main grids and matched controls, 50,032 local generations are
evaluated: 44,536 main-grid and frozen-Base readouts, 4,296 full-dose
Matched-SFT readouts, and 1,200 same-prefix-control readouts. An
answer-equivalence screen accepts clear matches, and GPT-5.6-Sol assigns
task-level labels to the remaining 17,178 responses using only the
problem, reference final answer, extracted answer, and final 500
whitespace-delimited response tokens. No worked solution or condition
metadata is shown. Claude Opus 4.8 independently evaluates a
deterministic 500-case sample stratified by model, readout, arm, seed,
and checkpoint, reaching 93.6% agreement (95% Wilson CI: 91.1–95.4%). A
blinded third pass resolves all 32 disagreements. Ambiguous responses
count as incorrect. Unless a caption states otherwise, 95%
seed-bootstrap intervals enumerate all $`3^{3}`$ ordered resamples of
the three prespecified training seeds and are descriptive. Stated
contrasts use unrounded seed means.

RQ4 otherwise uses the LoRA and optimizer configuration in
Table [B1](#A2.SS4 "B.4 Training ‣ Appendix B Data and common setup ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models"),
with a Thinking-ON teacher and student. Each run uses 48 updates with a
global batch size of 32, a linear learning-rate schedule from
$`5\times 10^{-6}`$, and checkpoints at Steps 12, 24, 36, and 48.
Standard OPSD rollouts use a 1,024-token cap, temperature 1.0, top-p
1.0, and no top-k truncation. Correction updates replace Standard OPSD
with continuation-only teacher-forced NLL on up to 1,024
verified-correction tokens after the audited error prefix, with no loss
on the prefix. All arms use three prespecified matched seeds.
Tables [G2a](#A7.SS2 "G.2 Protocol ‣ Appendix G RQ4: Expression ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")–[G2b](#A7.SS2 "G.2 Protocol ‣ Appendix G RQ4: Expression ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
list the batch schedules and evaluation conditions.

| Arm | Total updates | Correction updates | Correction share | Scheduled examples |
|----|----|----|----|----|
| Standard | 48 | 0 | 0% | 1,536 |
| Correction25 | 48 | 12 | 25% | 1,536 |
| Correction50 | 48 | 24 | 50% | 1,536 |
| Correction100 | 48 | 48 | 100% | 1,536 |

Table G2a: Fixed homogeneous-batch schedules. Non-correction updates use
Standard OPSD. Correction25 replaces updates 4, 8, …, 48. Correction50
replaces 2, 4, …, 48. Correction100 replaces every update. These
one-indexed positions are shared across arms, seeds, and scales.

| Readout | Input | Evaluation |
|----|----|----|
| Correction-target fit | Exact audited error prefix; teacher-forced verified correction | Question-averaged held-out per-token NLL; Steps 12/24/36/48 |
| Audited-prefix generation | Exact audited error prefix; no privileged support | Avg@8 accuracy; 16,384-token cap |
| Original-question restart | Original problem prompt; no privileged support | Avg@8 accuracy; 16,384-token cap |
| Explicit repair feedback | Error prefix + answer-free repair instruction | Step 48 Avg@8 accuracy; 16,384-token cap |
| Correction onset | Error prefix + first 32 non-answer-revealing correction tokens | Step 48 Avg@8 accuracy; 16,384-token cap |
| Broad mathematics | Original benchmark prompt; no privileged support | Step 48 Avg@12; 38,912-token cap; five-benchmark macro |

Table G2b: RQ4 evaluation conditions. Qwen3-4B uses 25 held-out
correction questions and Qwen3-8B uses 20. The 8B correction-onset
condition excludes one onset that reveals the answer and therefore uses
19 questions. All other local conditions use the full held-out split.
All evaluation generation uses temperature 1.0, top-p 1.0, and no top-k
truncation. Local frozen-Base readouts use one fixed evaluation.
Broad-mathematics Base rows average decoding seeds 0, 1, and 2.

Target fit improves without dose-ordered unsupported use. Greater
correction dose consistently lowers held-out NLL, but audited-prefix and
question-restart accuracy do not improve monotonically with dose. At

start-state-by-scale cells and exceeds it only by 0.42pp on the 8B
question restart.

### G.4 Step-48 generation endpoints

Tables [G4a](#A7.SS4 "G.4 Step-48 generation endpoints ‣ Appendix G RQ4: Expression ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")–[G4b](#A7.SS4 "G.4 Step-48 generation endpoints ‣ Appendix G RQ4: Expression ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
report the complete Step-48 endpoints plotted in
Figure [7](#S7 "7 RQ4: Expression ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models").
Tables [G5a](#A7.SS4 "G.4 Step-48 generation endpoints ‣ Appendix G RQ4: Expression ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")–[G5b](#A7.SS4 "G.4 Step-48 generation endpoints ‣ Appendix G RQ4: Expression ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
give the broad-mathematics results by benchmark.

Truncation falls without broad accuracy gains. Truncation is the only
broad generation metric that improves monotonically with correction dose
at both scales. Correction100 is 7.02pp below Standard at 4B and 3.39pp
below Standard at 8B on the macro average. The per-benchmark breakdown
shows that the broad decline is not an artifact of one benchmark. The
size varies across datasets, but no correction dose establishes a
consistent improvement over the matched frozen Base or Standard across
all five benchmarks.

### G.5 Matched controls

We test whether correction-training degradation is a generic consequence
of repeated SFT on a small dataset. Matched-SFT uses the same questions,
batch size, update count, fixed schedule, and row-wise target-token cap
as Correction100, but replaces each correction with the shortest
verifier-correct frozen-Base trajectory from the original question. An
additional Matched-SFT50 control replaces the same 24 batches as
Correction50 with the corresponding Base-correct targets while
preserving question order, batch position, and the row-wise target-token
cap. All arms use three prespecified seeds. In correction training, the
audited error prefix is prompt context and is excluded from the response
mask, so its tokens receive no loss. We then isolate that conditioning
context with a 4B same-prefix control. From the 100 Correction100
training prefixes, the frozen Base produces a verifier-correct,
non-truncated recovery for 76 usable prefixes. We preserve the
Correction100 order, repeat the 76-row sequence to the same 1,536
examples, and match the Base recovery and teacher correction target
lengths row by row. The two arms therefore differ only in continuation
content. The dose-matched and same-prefix controls in
Tables [G6c](#A7.SS5 "G.5 Matched controls ‣ Appendix G RQ4: Expression ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")–[G6d](#A7.SS5 "G.5 Matched controls ‣ Appendix G RQ4: Expression ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
are Qwen3-4B-only diagnostics.
Table [G6b](#A7.SS5 "G.5 Matched controls ‣ Appendix G RQ4: Expression ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
reports the Qwen3-8B Matched-SFT control.

Table G6d: Qwen3-4B same-prefix, length-matched control. The two arms
share identical error-prefix conditioning and row-matched target
lengths. Local accuracy uses the common 25-question held-out
audited-prefix evaluator rather than the 76-prefix training subset.
Entries are three-seed means. Audited-prefix brackets are 95%
seed-bootstrap intervals, and the other brackets are seed ranges.

Matched controls localize the 4B decline to the tested continuation. In
the same-prefix control, Base-recovery SFT reaches 87.50% local accuracy
versus 85.17% for the privileged-correction subset, a +2.33pp
paired-seed difference \[0.37, 3.89\]. At the 50% replacement dose,
Matched-SFT50 remains near Base at 60.68 Avg@12 and 3.49% truncation,
whereas Correction50 reaches 56.53 and 1.18%. Matching the replacement
count and positions does not reproduce either the large accuracy decline
or the strong termination shift. At full dose, Matched-SFT changes
five-benchmark Avg@12 by -0.71pp \[-1.18, +0.09\] at 4B and +0.86
\[+0.17, +1.55\] at 8B. Correction100 instead changes it by -7.25
\[-7.69, -6.94\] and -2.42 \[-3.28, -1.56\], showing that generic
repeated small-data SFT is insufficient to explain the decline. The
stricter same-prefix comparison in
Table [G6d](#A7.SS5 "G.5 Matched controls ‣ Appendix G RQ4: Expression ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
holds both the error prefix and target length fixed and therefore
localizes the additional broad degradation and stronger termination
change to the tested correction continuation rather than to the error
prefix alone.

Overall, correction dose reliably improves held-out target fit and
reduces truncation, but neither unsupported nor cue-supported generation
improves in a dose-ordered manner.

## Appendix H Discussion diagnostics

### H.1 Protocol

The diagnostics in
Tables [H1a](#A8.SS2 "H.2 Diagnostic values ‣ Appendix H Discussion diagnostics ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")–[H1f](#A8.SS2 "H.2 Diagnostic values ‣ Appendix H Discussion diagnostics ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
use reference-free Thinking-ON evaluation: Avg@12 over AIME 2024, AIME
2025, AIME 2026, HMMT November 2025, and HMMT February 2026, with a
38,912-token cap. Unless a diagnostic varies it, training uses a
Qwen3-4B student, a frozen same-size Thinking-ON teacher with the
privileged solution, Thinking-ON student rollouts, LoRA rank 64, global
batch size 32, learning rate $`5\times 10^{-6}`$, 1,024-token rollouts,
and 100 updates. Tables H1a and H1e average three prespecified training
seeds and report the seed range. Tables H1b, H1c, H1d, and H1f use one
training seed. All six diagnostics use the corresponding
three-decoding-seed Base mean as the common anchor, including the
single-training-seed diagnostics. Only the parameterization diagnostic
(Table H1a) appears in the main text, as
Figure [4](#S8.F4 "Figure 4 ‣ 8 Discussion ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")c.
Figure [1](#S1.F1 "Figure 1 ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
remains the primary factorial.

Diagnostic-specific variants. Table H1a compares Standard and Purified
OPSD under LoRA and full-parameter training on the 3,200-example
Hendrycks MATH set. The full-parameter BF16 FSDP runs use learning rate
$`10^{-6}`$, making this a practical parameterization control. Table H1b
varies the per-vocabulary-summand clip for Standard OPSD on the same
set: 0 gives exact FKL, and 0.05 is the released default. Table H1c
compares Standard, Purified, $`\beta`$-target, and $`\beta`$-target with
return-to-go variants on the 3,200-example OpenThoughts set. Purified
uses a centered PMI target with JSD loss. The $`\beta`$-OPSD variant
uses geometric mixing, with $`\gamma=0.99`$ for return-to-go. Table H1d
varies only the maximum training-response length among 1K, 4K, 8K, and
16K tokens on OpenThoughts. Evaluation retains the 38,912-token cap.
Table H1e compares a privileged-solution 1.7B self-teacher with
reference-free 4B and 8B teachers for a Qwen3-1.7B student on Hendrycks
MATH. Because teacher context and scale change jointly, this is not a
pure teacher-size ablation. Table H1f routes Hendrycks MATH Level 5
examples by Base competence (0/12, 1–5/12, 6–11/12, or 12/12 correct)
and trains Standard or Purified OPSD for 48 updates. Comparisons are
interpretable within each table because corpora, model scales, and
checkpoint grids differ across diagnostics.

Action diagnostics. The action-selection audit uses a Qwen3-4B model and
an 80-question OpenThoughts Frontier cohort, pooling Standard and
Purified Steps 0, 24, and 36. It compares oracle, privileged-target,
matched-random, and learned-critic selection over 135 branch events from
57 questions. Candidate actions are the policy and target top-1 tokens,
and critic evaluation uses five-fold problem-grouped cross-validation.
The single-seed sparse-replay pilot compares Oracle, FKL, and three
matched-random replay sets using one selected token for each of 33
training events per arm. Fold 0 holds out 30 states from 12 questions,
with four question-start samples per question and a 16K response cap.

three-decoding-seed Base anchor of 60.42%.

The controls alter trajectories and sometimes improve early checkpoints,
but every Standard or teacher-variation endpoint finishes below Base.
Purified sometimes ends modestly above Base, with its most repeatable
advantage being retention relative to Standard. Changing
parameterization, clipping, rollout horizon, teacher context and scale
jointly, or static competence routing does not prevent Standard's late
decline.

### H.3 Action selection

The action-selection audit pools six Qwen3-4B source cells from the
80-question OpenThoughts Frontier cohort: Standard and Purified targets
at their shared Step 0 and matched Step-24 and Step-36 checkpoints. At
135 top-FKL branch events from 57 problems, we force either the
current-policy top-1 token or the corresponding OPSD-target top-1 token,
remove the reference, and draw four continuations per action at
temperature 1.0, top-p 1.0, with top-k disabled and a 38,912-token
response cap. Exact-answer success averaged over the four continuations
defines each action's downstream value.

The learned critic is a frozen Qwen3-4B sequence-classification backbone
with only a binary value head trained. Its input contains only the
problem, prefix, and candidate action. Five folds are grouped by
problem, with separate held-out and validation folds. Training uses five
epochs, batch size 2, gradient accumulation 8, learning rate
$`10^{-3}`$, and maximum length 4,096, selecting the epoch by validation
AUROC. Routing uses the target action only when its predicted advantage
is positive.

For sparse replay, Oracle selects positive measured-advantage events,
FKL selects the highest-FKL events, and three Random selectors match
Oracle counts within source cell and fold. Fold 0 holds out 30 branch
states from 12 questions, leaving 33 one-token training events per arm.
Each fresh Base LoRA adapter uses rank 64, alpha 128, 24 updates,
effective batch size 8, learning rate $`5\times 10^{-6}`$ with cosine
decay, and loss only on the selected token. Question-start evaluation
draws four reference-free samples per held-out question at temperature
1.0 and top-p 1.0, with a 16K response cap.
Table [H2b](#A8.SS3 "H.3 Action selection ‣ Appendix H Discussion diagnostics ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
reports this single-seed pilot.
Table [H2a](#A8.SS3 "H.3 Action selection ‣ Appendix H Discussion diagnostics ‣ Privileged Information Does Not Guarantee Privileged Guidance: Auditing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Models")
gives the pooled action-selection results.

| Selector | Reference-free gain (pp) | Role |
|----|----|----|
| Hindsight oracle | +11.30 \[+8.15, +14.63\] | Non-deployable upper bound |
| Always privileged target | +2.04 | No routing |
| Matched random router | +1.12 | Matches 54.8% target coverage |
| Held-out learned critic | +0.56 \[-2.96, +4.07\] | Five-fold CV grouped by problem |
| High-FKL matched router | -1.30 | Same target-action count as critic |
| Low-FKL matched router | +3.15 | Same target-action count as critic |

Table H2a: Pooled action-selection results over 135 paired events from
57 questions. Brackets give problem-bootstrap 95% CIs, available for the
oracle and critic rows.

| Arm | Accuracy \[95% CI\] | $`\Delta`$ Base \[95% CI\] | Pass-any (%) | Trunc. (%) | Mean tokens |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|
| Frozen Base | 6.25 \[0.00, 12.50\] | +0.00 \[0.00, 0.00\] | 25.00 | 39.58 | 12,928.5 |
| Oracle replay | 8.33 \[0.00, 18.75\] | +2.08 \[-6.25, +10.42\] | 25.00 | 41.67 | 12,645.2 |
| FKL replay | 2.08 \[0.00, 6.25\] | -4.17 \[-10.42, +0.00\] | 8.33 | 39.58 | 12,607.2 |
